∎

Unfortunately, this formula does not resemble the formulas for
\varphi_{[k]}^{(k)}
given in equations (
2.1
), (
2.2
), and (
2.3
) in any meaningful way.
Indeed, if we were to expand out the definitions of
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.01274pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.01274pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.02548pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.01274pt}}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.02548pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.01274pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.01274pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.01274pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.02548pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.01274pt}}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.02548pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.01274pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.43115pt\hskip-2.43115pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.43115pt\hskip-2.43115pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu$}\hskip-2.43115pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.86232pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.43115pt}}\hskip-2.43115pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.86232pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.43115pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.12444pt\hskip-2.12444pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.12444pt\hskip-2.12444pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$}\hskip-2.12444pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.24889pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.12444pt}}\hskip-2.12444pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.24889pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.12444pt}}^{{}^{(k)}}_{\ell}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 12.39241pt\hskip-12.39241pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.39241pt\hskip-12.39241pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell x^{\ell-1}\xi$}\hskip-12.39241pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{9.99002pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 24.78482pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 12.39241pt}}\hskip-12.39241pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{8.99002pt}{\hbox{\rule{24.78482pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 12.39241pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 12.39241pt\hskip-12.39241pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.39241pt\hskip-12.39241pt\hbox{$\textstyle\ell x^{\ell-1}\xi$}\hskip-12.39241pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{9.49002pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 24.78482pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 12.39241pt}}\hskip-12.39241pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{8.49002pt}{\hbox{\rule{24.78482pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 12.39241pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 10.3855pt\hskip-10.3855pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 10.3855pt\hskip-10.3855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell x^{\ell-1}\xi$}\hskip-10.3855pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{7.49002pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 20.771pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.3855pt}}\hskip-10.3855pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{6.49002pt}{\hbox{\rule{20.771pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.3855pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 9.76398pt\hskip-9.76398pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 9.76398pt\hskip-9.76398pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell x^{\ell-1}\xi$}\hskip-9.76398pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.91666pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 19.52795pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 9.76398pt}}\hskip-9.76398pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.91666pt}{\hbox{\rule{19.52795pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 9.76398pt}}^{{}^{(k)}}
to
write them in terms of the multispace coordinates, the resulting formula is a mess of
elementary and homogeneous symmetric polynomials in
(x_{0},\dots,x_{k})
. This is demonstrated below in the expansion
of
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 5.25975pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{5.25975pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.59032pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.59032pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}^{{}^{(3)}}_{[3]}
:

Nevertheless, these formulas still satisfy a relationship in multispace space analogous to the recursion relationship (
2.1
) in smooth space.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let

∎

It is worth taking a moment to consider what Theorem
4.3
accomplishes. First of
all, the recursive formula (
4.3
) makes the practical computation of the infinitesimals for the action on the
multispace of curves tractable.
Note that this convenience is directly opposed to the preferred
methodology for jet space prolongation where we often used the expanded formula (
2.2
)
over the recursive formula (
2.1
).
Even for simple actions, the computation of the infinitesimals in multispace
according to the formulas described in Proposition
4.2
can involve some intense
determinants with highly non-trivial simplifications (see the end of Example
4.6
for a taste of the
types of rational expressions that appear here). Worse yet, at any given level
k
, there
are
(2k+1)
determinants to compute, and very few of the determinants computed for smaller values of

k
may be reused
to compute an infinitesimal for any larger
value of
k
. The formula given in Theorem
4.3
, on the other hand, allows us to take
advantage of the properties shown in Proposition
3.5
to write down explicit formulas for any
given
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 5.25975pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{5.25975pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.59032pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.59032pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}^{{}^{(k)}}_{[k]}
. Then, the computations for any value
k
will depend heavily on computations
at values
<k
. Practically speaking, this allows for the caching of some terms computed at values
smaller than
k
to save time in the computation at level
k
.

4.2
The coalescent limit

We are now ready to present the main result of this paper. As previously seen, it is possible to define an
operator on
M^{(n)}
that allows for the prolongation of an
n
-pointed curve to an
(n+1)
-pointed curve.
Moreover,
the infinitesimals for this action have a nearly identical recursive differential relationship to
those for the analogous action on jet space. Since the jet space appears as an embedded
smooth submanifold of multispace, this then suggests that the general recursion relations for prolonging a
group action on jet space are inherited from those on the larger multispace. More specifically, this suggests
that the coalescent limits of the prolongation formulas (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) in multispace should be exactly the prolongation formula (
2.1
) in the
jet space.

Theorem 4.4
.

\clim_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{n})\rightarrow x}\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 5.25975pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{5.25975pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.59032pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.59032pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}^{{}^{(k)}}_{[k]}=\varphi_{[k]}.

Proof.

We begin by observing the basic fact that

\clim_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{n})\rightarrow x}\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{5.72458pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$\textstyle u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 5.72458pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{5.72458pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.36403pt\hskip-2.36403pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.36403pt\hskip-2.36403pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle u$}\hskip-2.36403pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.72806pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.36403pt}}\hskip-2.36403pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.72806pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.36403pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.11519pt\hskip-2.11519pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.11519pt\hskip-2.11519pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u$}\hskip-2.11519pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.2304pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.11519pt}}\hskip-2.11519pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.2304pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.11519pt}}^{{}^{(k+1)}}\left(\frac{1}{x_{k+1}-x_{0}}(S^{k+1}-{\rm id})\bigl[\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 4.37502pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.1875pt}}\hskip-2.1875pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{6.94444pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.37502pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip and we proceed by induction. For our base-case, we may compute

\displaystyle\clim_{(x_{0},x_{1})\rightarrow x}\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.47221pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 3.33336pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 1.66667pt}}\hskip-1.66667pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.47221pt}{\hbox{\rule{3.33336pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 1.66667pt}}^{{}^{(0)}}\bigr]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\clim_{(x_0,\ldots,x_{k + 1})\rightarrow x}\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}^{^{(k + 1)}}_{[k + 1]}}{}\ =\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}x}\Bigl[\clim_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+1})\rightarrow x}\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 5.25975pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{5.25975pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.59032pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.59032pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}^{{}^{(k)}}_{[k]}\Bigr]-u^{(k+1)}\xi^{(1)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\clim_{(x_0,\ldots,x_{k + 1})\rightarrow x}\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}^{^{(k + 1)}}_{[k + 1]}}{}\ =\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}x}\bigl[\varphi_{[k]}\bigr]-u^{(k+1)}\xi^{(1)}\overset{\eqref{eq: 1d-recursion-smooth}}{=}\varphi_{[k+1]},

as desired.
∎

Corollary 4.5
.

\clim_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+1})\rightarrow x}\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 6.54167pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{6.54167pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.27083pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.62987pt\hskip-2.62987pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{4.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 5.25975pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}\hskip-2.62987pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.01389pt}{\hbox{\rule{5.25975pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.62987pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.29515pt\hskip-2.29515pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varphi$}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{3.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern 4.59032pt\kern-0.4pt\kern-0.4pt\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}\hskip-2.29515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.59032pt}{0.4pt}}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.29515pt}}^{{}^{(k+1)}}_{[k+1]}=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}x}\left[\varphi_{[k]}\right]-u^{(k+1)}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}x}\xi.

Importantly, Theorem
4.4
tells us that the
k
-th order multispace
prolongation of the action on some curve
(x,u)
sampled at
points
\left\{x_{0},\dots,x_{n}\right\}
yields the equivalence class containing the
k
-th order jet at
each of these points. Put more concisely, multicontact equivalent curves behave well under
multispace prolongation of the group action.
This fact is not obvious from the definitions. Recall that the lift from
M^{(n)}
to

M^{(n+1)}
is not necessarily unique, so it is entirely possible that the action could lift different
sections of the curve into disjoint equivalence classes. Based on the construction of this space, there is
no obvious reason to suspect that this splitting would happen, but the details still needed to be checked
rigorously.

4.3
Examples

With the theory out of the way, it is time to consider a couple of examples for computing the infinitesimals of a given group action.

Example 4.6
.

Consider the elementary group action of
\mathbb{R}^{*}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
given by

(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{u})=\bigl(\lambda^{-1}x,\lambda u\bigr).

In the jet case, the infinitesimals are given by

\xi=-x,\qquad\varphi=u\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varphi_{[k]}=(k+1)u^{(k)}.

Computing for the multispace case, we find that

Example 4.7
.

Let us now consider another quintessential example: rotation in the plane. This is the action of
{\rm SO}(2)
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
given by

\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{x}\\
\widetilde{u}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos(\theta)&-\sin(\theta)\\
\sin(\theta)&\cos(\theta)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}x\\
u\end{pmatrix}.

In the jet case, the first few infinitesimals are

\displaystyle\xi=-u,\qquad\varphi=x,\qquad\varphi_{[1]}=1+\bigl(u^{(1)}\bigr)^{2},

\displaystyle\varphi_{[2]}=3u^{(1)}u^{(2)},\qquad\varphi_{[3]}=3\bigl(u^{(2)}\bigr)^{2}+4u^{(1)}u^{(3)}.

For the multispace case, we obtain
\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.1875pt\hskip-2.1875pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.1875pt\hskip-2.1875pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\xi$}\hskip-2.1875pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{7.94444pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern The first few prolonged infinitesimals can now be computed using our recursion relations (
4.3
)

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 3.27083pt\hskip-3.27083pt\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}\hskip-3.27083pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.30554pt}{\hbox{\rule{0.4pt}{1.1pt}\kern , we know that

5
Conclusion and future work

At the onset of this paper, we set out to answer three questions with regard to a multispace prolongation of the action of a group on a curve.
We defined an operator
\frac{\Delta}{\Delta x}
on the multispace that behaves analogously to the behavior of
\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}x}
on jet space. With this operator in hand, we then showed that the natural way of defining a prolongation of the action on multipointed curves behaves well under the coalescent limit, in the sense that it converges to the expected smooth analogue.

There are two natural ways in which we might expand on this work. The first would be to investigate the
existence of Noether-like theorems within multispace. To this end, the first step would be to determine the
appropriate analogue for the characteristic for a vector field acting on
M^{(n)}
. The natural guess would
be to take

; however, this choice does not allow

) as would be desired in order to prove a true-to-form Noether theorem (cf.

9

).
7
7

7

The characteristic
formulation is necessary for Lagrangian and variational methods, but it is possible
to obtain Noether-type theorems without using these methods. See
[
4
,
17
]
.

The other natural extension would be to try to determine whether these formulas still hold in the case of a multispace of more than one independent variable. The tricky thing here would be the definition of the divided difference equations. While it is generally possible to find a polynomial surface passing through a given set of points, the issue when it comes to defining a multispace in the way that we have here is determining (i) the uniqueness of the interpolating polynomial and (ii) the existence of the coalescent limit. Marí Beffa and Mansfield managed to construct such a multispace that accomplishes both of these in
[
11
]
, and work by Olver in
[
15
]
seems to suggest that a more general multispace could be constructed, but practical computation of a prolonged action on the more general space is easier said than done and is currently under investigation by the author of this paper.
